# Model Plane build: depth register

**Building strategy (owner's order, 2026-10-23).** Build one wave at a time. A slice is built to the plan row's code and the unit tests the row names, then committed. Everything that is depth is written down here and is not chased while the wave is being built: live proofs, rebuilds, database harness runs, hostile reviews, edge cases, hardening, owner decisions, cleanup. When a wave is fully built the build stops. The depth findings are then prioritised with the owner, the wave is tested, and only then does the next wave start (or depth work is scheduled first).

Waves follow the plan (`docs/plans/model-plane-chat-build-plan-2026-10-14.md`, section 3): **Wave 1 = WS01 to WS12** ("make what exists trustworthy and provable"), **Wave 2 = WS13 to WS27** ("close the agent-loop and governance gaps"). Waves 3 to 5 are epics in plan section 3.3 and are not in the queue.

## How an item is logged

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ID | `D1-nnn` for Wave 1, `D2-nnn` for Wave 2 |
| Slice | the slice that raised it, or `cross` |
| Kind | `live-proof`, `test-gap`, `risk`, `follow-up`, `decision`, `cleanup` |
| P | **P0** a hole in authority, ZDR, money or data already visible in committed code; **P1** needed before the behaviour can be switched on or the wave called tested; **P2** quality or test gap; **P3** cleanup |
| Status | `open`, or `done <commit>` once closed in a depth phase |

A batch's `depth_findings` and the verifier's findings are appended here by the lead after the batch is committed.


## Standard depth passes at the end of a wave

1. Rebuild every service the wave changed (Model Plane images, BFF gateway, frontend on a second dev server) with `.plan-work/execution/tools/recreate-with-live-env.sh`; check boot logs.
2. Run the session-core DB harness, `scripts/verify-durable-layer.sh`, the capability-core integration tests and every `*-proof.sh` the wave added.
3. Hostile review (Opus) of the strict slices (authority, audit, ZDR, money, data-loss) built without one, then a fix round for what it finds (the table at the end lists them).
4. The live proofs below, then Playwright and manual checks for the SPA slices.
5. Triage the pre-existing failures (cross section) and decide which to fix.
6. Priorities with the owner; update the status of each item.

## Testing: light during the build, one test pass at the end of the wave (owner's order, 2026-10-23)

No big test run stops the build. The rule has three tiers:

| Tier | When | What | Blocks the build? |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | inside every slice | only the slice's own unit tests (the commands in its plan row), by the implementing agent | no |
| B | once, when the wave's last buildable slice is committed | the **wave test pass**: workspace compile (`cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `go build ./...` per Go service, `tsc`), the unit suites (`cargo test --lib` for execution-core, model-gateway, inference-core, mp-contracts; `cargo test -p session-core --all-targets` without the ignored DB tests; `go test ./...` per Go service; `pnpm exec vitest run`, `pnpm lint`, the BFF `cargo test`), and the static proof runners (`durable-loop-proof.sh static`). Run in the background and time-boxed (stop a suite that runs past 20 minutes and log it) while the wave report is written. | no: a red test is logged here as a finding (P0 or P1 when it shows a broken slice, else P2) and the owner decides at the stop |
| C | after the owner has prioritised the depth | everything that needs docker: the session-core DB harness, `verify-durable-layer.sh`, capability-core integration tests, rebuilds and live proofs, Playwright, the hostile reviews | it is the depth work itself |

The pre-existing failures listed under Cross-wave (D0-003) are not a reason to stop: they are compared against by name, not fixed in the build.

### Round-2 leftovers and integration notes (2026-10-23)

| ID | Slice | Kind | P | Finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1-025 | WS04-S4, WS14-S2 | risk | **P0** | With `MODEL_GATEWAY_DURABLE_PLAN_GRANT` OFF (the default) the gRPC `ExitPlanMode` does not compare the caller's `session_id` with the run's own thread: the owner of any run can plant an in-memory grant (up to `danger_full_access`) on another thread of the org on that replica, and `RUN_PLAN_MODE_EXITED` names it. The path exists since WS14-S2a. Fix: call `read_run_detail` regardless of the flag, refuse a `session_id` that differs from `detail.thread_id`, pass `detail.thread_id` into the handler (the flag-on path already does). Found by the round-2 re-review; not fixed under the wave strategy. | resolved 2026-10-26 (Wave 1 depth: content-free log fields and the ExitPlanMode thread check) |
| D1-026 | WS04-S4 | risk | P1 | `with_local_fallback` consults memory only when Session Core holds no grant, so a newer, narrower in-memory grant (a waived approval, or a transient `SetRunMode` failure) loses to an older, broader durable grant for up to 24 h. Fix: stamp the memory entry and compare with a `granted_at` from `GetThreadGrant`, or take the narrower rung. | resolved 2026-10-26 (Wave 1 depth packet ws04-ws05-ws12-gateway-grant-fallback-and-probe-scope) |
| D1-027 | WS04-S4 | risk | P2 | `RUN_PLAN_MODE_EXITED` (rung, justification, plan id) is published before the `SetRunMode` result is known, so a refused write leaves an audit event naming a grant that exists nowhere; `ExitPlanModeResponse` has no `persisted` field. | open |
| D1-028 | WS04-S4 | risk | P2 | `set_run_mode_retryable` retries `Code::Unknown` but `set_run_mode_failure_is_transient` excludes it, so an approval that failed with `Unknown` is retried four times and then kept nowhere (stranded, fails closed). Use one predicate. | open |
| D1-029 | WS05-S4 | test-gap | P2 | The second-round fixes (built-in classes learned by ZDR and waived runs, merge-style hydrate, fail-closed on transport errors and 5xx) were not re-reviewed: the re-review was stopped to save time. | open |
| D1-030 | cross | live-proof | P1 | The Model Plane paths of `quarryv2-next` were imported (commit `5ca40750`); the Rust compile check of model-gateway and the browser-broker Go tests, the Postgres store tests and the end-to-end harness have not been run. A full merge of `quarryv2-next` (Quarry, Ingestion, Frontend parts included) is still ahead and conflicts today in about 8 non-Model-Plane files. | open |
| D1-031 | WS05-S4 | follow-up | P2 | `MCP_DO_TOOLS=all` stays blocked; the two-week shadow soak and the deletion of the name heuristics (WS05-S6) come later. | open |

| D1-543 | WS03-S8 | risk | P1 | Nothing writes the executor markers on round folds yet, so with SESSION_CORE_CRASH_PARK_ENABLED on the worker refuses every fold as route_unrecorded; the executor must write parks-so-far (a resumed run's count too, or the park cap is bypassed), route model, connection id and child ceiling on round folds of run_parkable loops only, and never on an approval continuation or client/MCP-tool run, or crash-park holds runs nothing can resume for the 24 h TTL. | resolved 2026-10-26 (Wave 1 depth packet ws01-ws02-ws03-crash-park-round-markers) |
| D1-544 | WS03-S8 | risk | P1 | Zombie loop: a loop silent for more than the 15 min deadline plus the 15 min grace is crash parked and can then be continued while still alive; its later fold save goes stale and the writer fails but the loop keeps going (read-only calls, final answer), so two loops can answer one run; the thread watermark only catches an answer already appended, so the executor must stop a run_parkable loop at its first stale/closed save before any further inference, append or receipt. | resolved 2026-10-26 (Wave 1 depth packet ws01-ws02-ws03-crash-park-round-markers) |
| D1-545 | WS03-S8 | risk | P1 | No UI or button resumes a park, so with crash park on a dead run is now held up to SESSION_CORE_PARK_TTL_SECONDS (default 86400) with no thread explanation instead of ending outcome_unknown at about 15 min; the owner has to decide the TTL (or a fixed notice) before flip (4). | open |
| D1-546 | WS03-S8 | decision | P1 | The S8-B go-ahead and design are open owner decisions in the ledger (three design reviews found critical defects in the earlier designs); this half follows the v4 invariants that fit the owned files (marker-gated, grace plus heartbeat push, thread lock, watermark re-check at claim) but has not had its own independent review. | open |
| D1-547 | WS03-S8 | live-proof | P1 | The park_crashed SQL, the claim re-check, the SKIP LOCKED thread lock and the heartbeat push are source-verified only; none ran against Postgres (five ignored DB tests written), and the live proof (docker kill execution-core past the deadline, park_not_due, resume by continue, TTL expiry) needs the executor half first. | open |
| D1-548 | WS03-S8 | risk | P1 | The thread_watermark stamp lives in fold extensions and is carried forward by any resume that clones the fold, so a later executor-written park fold (credential window, provider exhausted) on a crash-resumed run keeps the old stamp; claim() keys its thread re-check on the stamp alone, so it would close that legitimate park when the thread has moved since, ending the run outcome_unknown. Strip the key when the executor writes a park fold, or key the claim re-check on park_reason crash_interrupted, before the switch is flipped. | closed 2026-10-26 (not a defect: Executor folds never carry thread_watermark: fold::build_fold starts from a fresh extensions map (execution-core runtime_loop/fold.rs:477-49) |
| D1-549 | WS03-S8 | risk | P1 | Executor markers are not written on round folds, so with SESSION_CORE_CRASH_PARK_ENABLED on every fold is refused route_unrecorded; the executor must write the four park keys (including a resumed run's park count) on run_parkable round folds only, never on approval continuations or client/MCP-tool runs. | resolved 2026-10-26 (Wave 1 depth packet ws01-ws02-ws03-crash-park-round-markers) |

| D1-689 | WS04-S8 | risk | P1 | Crash ordering. If the owner drops the message from its queue and dies before session-core marks the row withdrawn, the row stays live for a message the agent never read, and a run-end resend under the same client message id would answer a retracted message. The mark must come first, and the queue entry is released only after it is acknowledged. | open |

| D1-808 | WS02-S8 | cost | P1 | Measured 2026-10-27 on claude-sonnet-5-5: six turns in two threads, consecutive turns 8-15 s apart: cache_read_tokens 0 on every turn, cache_write_tokens equal to the whole input on every turn, cost-core reason prefix_changed on every follow-up. The legacy prompt order puts per-turn blocks (grounding, session-core's context block) among the leading system messages, and the stable-prefix order is off for Claude by design (prompt_context.rs CacheRoute::of_turn). Every Anthropic turn pays the cache-write premium on its whole input and never the read discount. Owner decision in docs/plans/model-plane-wave1-owner-decisions-2026-10-28.md. | open |

| D1-810 | WS04-S2 | follow-up | P2 | Residuals of the D1-229 fix (Do runs stop on Stop): (1) a stopped Do run's usage is never ledgered, because the abort drops the RunAgent response and AGENT_USAGE is published only on an Ok answer (contradicts the 2026-10-22 ledger entry); either Execution Core reads its own cancel latch and returns usage, or the gap is accepted. (2) ProducerLiveness now reports a Do request id as live, so a resume of one holds a tail with no buffered frames until the run ends instead of a 404. (3) A Stop between connected and spawn_run_dispatch still starts one provider round before the first tick aborts it. | D1-812 | WS01-S3 | defect | P1 | Found by round P (D1-805, 2026-10-28): a fresh auth-core schema cannot be built from the committed files. docker-entrypoint.sh (~:181) still lists migrations/init_better_auth.sql, deleted in 932b30d26 (2026-09-23); drizzle/0002 changes column types without USING; team_member is created in drizzle 0002 and dropped in 0005, while migrations/gdpr_hard_delete.sql still deletes from it, so on a schema built from today's files its EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS handler would turn a GDPR hard delete into success:false. Owner decision: commit a base schema (or restore the file as the bootstrap migration). | open |
| D1-813 | WS01-S7.code | defect | P1 | Found by the ZDR live proof (2026-10-28): a session-core image built from a clean checkout cannot boot on the dev stack's session_core database. `.gitattributes` (94ce1952c, 2026-10-23) sets `eol=lf` for the migrations, so a fresh checkout (a new worktree, CI, a Linux release candidate) has LF bytes, but every `_sqlx_migrations.checksum` on the dev database was recorded from CRLF working copies (the main checkout and the `epic-shaw` worktree still hold `w/crlf` copies; migration 1: the stored SHA-384 equals the CRLF rendering, not the LF file). The rebuilt image at 0b5d2b7b6 crash-looped with "migration 1 was previously applied but has been modified" (about 6 minutes, rolled back to d67c5a43f). Every image built from the older checkouts keeps working, which hides it. Fix (owner/integrator, one step, coordinated with whoever builds session-core next): re-checksum `_sqlx_migrations` to the LF bytes after checking each file differs only in line endings (pg_dump first), then renormalize the old checkouts' working copies (`git add --renormalize` or a fresh checkout); until then build session-core only from a CRLF checkout, or the signed WS01-S12 candidate will fail against this database the same way. | open |
| D1-814 | WS01-S7.code | follow-up | P3 | Harness: the Playwright `e2e` project signs in from its base URL (default `http://localhost:5199`, tests/e2e/auth.setup.ts sends Origin = baseURL), but the dev stack's auth-core trusts only :5173 and :3185 (BETTER_AUTH_TRUSTED_ORIGINS), so setup fails 403 INVALID_ORIGIN although the BFF's own allowlist has :5199. The ZDR proof signed in with Origin :5173 and handed the cookies to Playwright (`--no-deps`). Fix: add :5199 to the dev auth-core's trusted origins (Control Plane env, main checkout) or let auth.setup.ts take an `E2E_AUTH_ORIGIN`. | open |
| D1-815 | WS01-S7.code | defect | P1 | Found by the ZDR live proof (2026-10-28): the composer sends web search on every turn by default (`readBrowseWebPreference`: on unless `verevon.chat.browseWeb.v1` is '0'), and nothing turns it off for an org with ZDR on, while model-gateway refuses web search under ZDR (`zdr_feature_not_supported`, feature `web_search`, action `turn_off_feature`). So every default turn from a ZDR org is refused, also after a provider is attested, and pass 2 sent from the SPA cannot be answered. The composer already reads the org's ZDR for Temporary chat. Fix: default web search off (and say why) while the active org has ZDR on, or leave the web_search tool out of a ZDR turn; the gateway refusal stays as the guard. | resolved 2026-10-28 (DashboardComposer reads the org ZDR intent and, under org ZDR or Temporary chat, sends no web search and shows Søk disabled with the reason; 4 new composer tests, mutation-checked; live: a default turn from the ZDR-TEST org went out with browse_web false and was refused zdr_unavailable, zero delta; evidence live-proof-wave1-zdr-2026-10-28.txt part 2) |
| D1-816 | cross | follow-up | P2 | Seen while closing D1-225 (2026-10-28): `node docs/compliance/evidence/tools/check-doc-codes.mjs` exits 1 at HEAD 0b5d2b7b6 with four failures that predate this session (identical with and without its ZDR.md edits): (1) and (2) `doc-codes.json` points `zdr_feature_not_supported` at model-gateway `sse.rs` and `zdr_durable_mutation_forbidden` at `http_routes.rs`, but both now live in `model-gateway/src/zdr_posture.rs` (the first was emitted live on 2026-10-28); (3) ZDR.md names `zdr_turn_registers_no_run_control_listener`, which is in no registry list; (4) the registry still lists `zdr_connection_not_supported` as designed while ZDR.md 5.9 says it is built (Z-00). Fix: repoint the two source paths, register (3) as a term, move (4) to built; then the checker gates again. | open |

| D2-976 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | AcquireLease for a thread scope accepts any scope_id from any user with a sandbox bearer and never checks it against session-core's thread ownership, so a user can create workspace rows and blobs under another user's thread id in the same org. S5 refuses to erase a shared id, which also blocks the victim's own erase. The ownership check belongs in acquireThread before default-on. | open |
| D2-977 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | The blob prefix cas/thread/<org>/<thread>/ carries no owner, and blobs are uploaded before their rows exist, so a non-owner erase could remove a first write whose header is not yet there. An owner-scoped prefix or an erasing tombstone is needed. | open |
| D2-978 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | Rows are erased before blobs, so a blob-delete failure leaves unreachable blobs with no record to retry from. A durable erasure tombstone or retry queue is needed before default-on for GDPR completeness. | open |
| D2-979 | WS22-S5 | follow-up | P1 | With SANDBOX_MANAGER_THREAD_WORKSPACE off, neither the sweeper nor DELETE runs, so rows and blobs written while it was on are neither expired nor erasable after a rollback. A retention-only path is needed before default-on. | open |
| D2-980 | WS22-S5 | follow-up | P1 | The WS17 thread-delete hook is not wired: session-core's delete_thread must call DELETE /v1/threads/{id}/workspace with the user's own bearer, because sandbox-manager refuses service principals for this RPC. This gates default-on. | open |
| D2-981 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | Blobs live under cas/thread/<org>/<thread>/ with no owner, and the shared-id check only sees rows. Any org member whose sandbox bearer passes DELETE for a thread id with no rows yet removes another user's blobs that were uploaded before their rows were written (uploads precede ApplyThreadWorkspaceChanges), and nothing checks thread ownership against session-core. | open |
| D2-982 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | AcquireLease and DeleteThreadWorkspace accept any thread id without a session-core ownership check, so a user can plant rows under another user's thread id. ErrThreadShared then blocks the victim's own erase and keeps the thread from retention forever, logging only a warning. | open |
| D2-983 | WS22-S5 | risk | P1 | Rows are erased before blobs, and a failed blob delete in the sweeper is only logged. The rows are gone, so no later pass finds the thread again and its blobs stay forever (a GDPR completeness gap) until a tombstone or retry queue exists. | open |

| D2-1085 | WS14-S4 | risk | P1 | answerPendingQuestion and skipPendingQuestion write after their await with no thread check. | D0-010 | WS20-S5 | risk | P1 | Step-time re-verification of the stored-consent contract is not built: execution-core's step verifier checks only Control's signed claims and never recomputes `scheduled_contract::digest(contract_json)` against `claims.contract_digest` (no `scheduled_step_contract_rejects_digest_mismatch` or `_rejects_expired` test), session-core's `ResolveScheduledStepAuthority` and `GetScheduledStepContext` return empty contract fields, and user-core's step decision handler takes no contract fields (it must read them from the prepared authority, never from the orchestrator's body). The run-execution decision carries the contract; the step decision stays v1. `step_loop.go` was edited outside the slice's file list. Build this before `SCHEDULE_CONTRACT_ENABLED` or `EXECUTION_CORE_SCHEDULED_CONTRACT_REQUIRED` goes on; the latter refuses every v1 step decision while on. | open |

| D0-014 | WS20-S6 | risk | P1 | The scheduled Do lane's ZDR clause is not wired: `execute_scheduled_agent_step` builds the caller with `zdr: false`, `org_zdr: None`, `zdr_waiver: None` and `run_request` hard-codes `zdr: false`, so `refuses_retention` can never fire. `ScheduledStepContext` (runs.proto) needs the run's zdr and waiver at new tags, session-core must serve them, and grpc.rs must build the caller from them. The lane is unreachable today only because session-core does not serve the contract either (`contract_json` must be stored as exact text in the StartScheduledRun metadata and served at `get_scheduled_step_context`). Do NOT set `EXECUTION_SCHEDULED_AGENT_ENABLED` before both land. Also owed: the `scheduled_do_run` row in `zdr-posture-matrix.json` (refused for every ZDR and waived cell, with its pin and vendored copies), and the choice between CompleteRunActivity and FailRunActivity for a non-completed agent-lane step. | open |
